Crash curve: nothing below 8 m/s, fraction = ((v - 8) / 20.5)^1.8 of the reference hull, and a hit up to 56 km/h never takes more than 12 % of max hp (`CRASH_BUMP_MAX`). A car the player drives that holds the throttle without moving for 1.5 s backs itself out for 1.1 s (`Vehicle.step_drive`, `wedge_count`). The camera arm ignores the pooled prop colliders, so `CameraRig._prop_avoid` pushes a camera that touches one aside with a sphere query. The hack site is drawn as a pad (`ContractVisuals.set_pad_style`, uniforms `inner`, `fill_a`, `ripple` of the ring shader); `show_marker` resets it. The encounter director opens at 18 s with a squad 11-21 m away.

## Scanned world materials (wave 99)
Poly Haven CC0 scans packed into the existing surface slots (`SurfaceTextures.WORLD`, `world_<name>_a/_n.webp`, 1024 px): district road array (`srf_roadv_*`, `SurfaceTextures.road_array`), the district sidewalk array (scanned layers, `WALK_SCAN_LAYERS`), facade `brick` / `wall`, prop `metal` / `corr`, and the interior surface samplers (`scan_*`, `SurfaceTextures.apply_interior`). `MaterialLib` (wave 22, per-kind raw sets) is superseded for the city by this path and stays as a fallback. LOW: one albedo tap through `srf_lo` (lite ground, building_core), arrays at 512 px; MED / HIGH: albedo + normal taps as before. `QT_SCANNED=0` = procedural. Sky: three equirect probes (`sky_probe_{dusk,night,overcast}.png`) blended in `sky.gdshader` by `DayNight.probe_weights`. Pipeline and credits: HANDOFF "Wave 99", `godot/tools/materials/`, `art_sources/polyhaven_pbr/`.


## Real audio recordings (round 4, open-source sound)
- **What changed:** the weapon shots (all classes), impacts, explosions, car crashes, footsteps per surface, engines, UI cues, reloads, hit markers and the six ambience beds are built from real CC0 recordings (OpenGameArt: The Free Firearm Sound Library, Kenney, qubodup, number99, Snabisch ...). Table of every sample with URL, author and licence: `docs/AUDIO_SOURCES.md`; credits in `docs/THIRD_PARTY.md`; each pack keeps `LICENSE.txt` + `SOURCE.md` in `art_sources/audio_<group>/` next to the 184 clips the game uses (3.6 MB: mono 44.1 kHz OGG, loops FLAC).
- **Pipeline (offline, reproducible, bit-exact in `verify_asset_build.sh`):** `tools/audio/real_clips.py` (clip table and the one-time cut from the downloaded packs; `python3 real_clips.py <raw folder>`) -> `tools/audio/make_real.py` (builds `assets/audio/*` from the committed clips: real transient + our own sub-bass thump and a presence boost for gun classes, Kenney lasers for the pulse classes, a synthesised sub fall under real booms, circular re-pitch for engine loops, crossfaded 24 s beds; mastered with `make_redesign.master` to the peak and K-weighted loudness of the sound it replaces, numbers in `real_levels.py` taken from the synthesised originals so the `Sfx` mix table and `check_audio.py` stay as tuned; writes `assets/audio/real_manifest.json`) -> `shrink_audio.py`. `tools/build_assets.sh` runs it after every synthesising generator; `tools/audio/write_audio_sources.py` regenerates the provenance files.
- **Takes:** repeating sounds now have several different recordings (`shot_mini` and `shot_burst` x4, `shot_twin`/`shot_rev`/`shot_scatter`/`shot_flux` x3, `step_asphalt` 8, `step_metal` 10, ...); `Sfx.variants_of` finds `<name>_<k>`; their `.import` files copy the sibling's (PCM, never QOA: the tests read the WAV). Still synthesised: `tyre_squeal`, `engine_hover_*`, quantum ability sounds, voices, music, sirens, pickups.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_audio_real_samples.gd` (provenance files, all weapons resolve, takes differ, loud, broadband crack, families and loops through `Sfx`, size budget, every hitscan weapon plays its own recording in the real scene). `test_audio.gd` windows for short real footsteps / UI / whizz lowered (comment there). Measured, not listened: nobody has heard it on a phone yet.

## Open-source vehicles and guns (round 4)
CC0 models, converted and wired with the older art as fallback (credits: docs/THIRD_PARTY.md, licences and URLs: art_sources/kenney_car_kit, art_sources/open_weapons; test: tests/test_open_models.gd; sheet: docs/shots/slice/open_models_before_after.jpg).
- Vehicles: `tools/open_models/convert_vehicles.py` (bpy, `.venv-tools`) turns 14 Kenney Car Kit models into `assets/models/ov_*.glb`: palette colours baked into vertex colours (no texture memory), faces sorted into Body (paint, re-tinted per car) / Details / Headlights / Taillights (synthesised when the kit has none), wheels as separate nodes at the axles with the real track, a piecewise stretch of the body (round arches kept, wheelbase 0.6 x length), one subdivision so crash dents have vertices to push, Body_LOD1 (40 %). 1.4k to 2.4k triangles per car. `VehicleSpecs.SPECS[kind]["ov"]` names the model; `model_names()` puts it first, except kinds flagged `ov_low` (sedan, sport, suv, taxi, van, truck): their older, smoother art stays first above LOW and the open model serves LOW. Kinds that had no art (compact, interceptor, ambulance) or are new (luxury, racer, delivery, garbage, firetruck, all in STREAM_KINDS) always use it. `QT_OPEN_MODELS=0` forces the old art (before captures, test_vehicle_models).
- Weapons: `tools/open_models/convert_weapons.py` (pure python) writes `assets/models/ow_<id>.glb` for 12 weapons (muzzle at the origin, barrel -Z, bore axis y 0, real length). `WeaponModels.mesh()` tries `_open()` first and falls back to the procedural builder; grips and reach tables are unchanged (the length table in the converter matches the old meshes). Weapon pictures in menus (WeaponIcons) use the same meshes.

## Map freeze fix, full-screen map, screen watchdog (round 4; tests/test_map_no_freeze.gd)
- Cause found (reproduced in the real scene): while the map was open Game.state was `paused`, and `Hud._input` / `TouchControls._input` / story / tutorial / photo handlers kept reacting to every tap and key. A tap on the map pressed the hidden pause-menu button under it (RESUME resumed play under the map, MAP / OPTIONS / LOADOUT opened screens invisibly), Esc resumed the game, and the pause screen under the map was redrawn at 20 Hz (first draw of it was a 0.4-0.9 s hitch on the desktop, several seconds on a phone). Also: the tap that opened the map (a minimap tap) fell through to the map and placed a waypoint; the draw issued about 1500 rects per redraw at 5 Hz (100 ms at zoom 1).
- Fix: `MapScreen.up` (static) is checked first by Hud, TouchControls (a finger lift still passes), Story, Tutorial and PhotoMode; the map handles Esc, `ui_cancel`, pad B and the phone back key itself; `MapScreen.open()` sets `up` before the state change and calls `Hud.map_toggled` (the pause screen and HUD are hidden under the map, restored on close); `_process` closes the map if the state is no longer `paused`; pointer input is ignored for INPUT_QUIET (0.25 s) after opening; chevrons are bounded (MAX_CHEVRONS, off-screen segments skipped, non-finite points dropped by `clean_polyline`), NaN / INF zoom, pan and waypoint are refused.
- Draw cost: tint per block, building footprints and fog are `ArrayMesh` layers (`rect_mesh`, one `draw_mesh` each, rebuilt only when the lots or the charted cells change): a draw is about 15-20 ms in a debug build (was 100).
- Bigger map: the view is the whole screen (`_rect` = map control, `span()` = metres top to bottom, `ppm()`, `extent()` = metres across the screen); zoom 1 fits the city to the height, a wide phone shows the outskirts grid at the sides; `fit_route()` (button GOAL, and on open when a route exists) frames hero and objective together (zoom 1-2.6); side panels are opaque; names are clamped inside the screen; every badge reserves its box before the names are written.
- `ScreenWatchdog` (scripts/ui/screen_watchdog.gd, child of the Hud, layer 60): after 5 s of real time without progress (state, map pan / zoom / tap / waypoint, screen signature) on a closable screen (map, photo, pause sub-screen, vendor, TEST menu) a 64 px+ BACK button appears and closes the top screen. The plain pause menu and the title are never "trapped". `force_idle(secs)`, `trapped()`, `shown()`, `press()` for tests.
- Not reproduced: an infinite loop in clustering / route code (none exists with finite input; the bounds above are defensive). The measured headless costs are desktop numbers; the phone hitch could not be measured here.

## Weapons with pictures and numbers (owner round 4; tests/test_weapon_cards.gd; sheets docs/shots/slice/weapon_cards_*.jpg)
- **Pictures.** `tools/icons/build_icons.sh` (icon_maker.tscn, software Vulkan under xvfb, then `post.py`) renders every weapon (`w_<id>`), throwable and gadget (`g_<id>`, small stand-in solids), vehicle kind (`v_<kind>`, the real `Vehicle` node) and enemy type (`e_<type>`, the glb tinted) from its own model to a transparent PNG in `assets/icons/` (79 files, about 330 KB, lossless import, committed: the game needs no renderer for them). `ItemIcons` loads them on first use and caches (`weapon / gear / vehicle / enemy / any(id)`, `draw_tex`, `draw_weapon`); `WeaponIcons.draw` now draws the picture and falls back to the flat silhouette. Used by the weapon wheel, the loadout tiles, the HUD plate and weapon pop-up, the WEAPONS catalogue (a button dict key `pic` = {p, id, r, a} is drawn by Hud over the plate). `tools/icons/sheet.py` makes a contact sheet.
- **Numbers.** `WeaponStats.card(id, def)` (pure): damage per shot and pellets, rpm, magazine / spare / reload, effective and maximum range, blast and well radius, hip and aimed spread (degrees), recoil, penetration, lock-on, and six 0..1 bars (damage, fire rate, range, accuracy, control, penetration). `damage_at(id, dist)` is the range falloff PlayerWeapon applies (full to `falloff_from`, down to 25 % at `range`); `blast_share(d, r)` the explosion falloff (1.0 at the centre, 0.4 at the edge); `where(id)` the routes (starter, crate, mission, boss, enemy drop, shop); `crates_of(id)`.
- **WEAPONS tab** (`WeaponsScreen`, the weapon pages of the HERO hub: `ShopScreen.weapon_page` 1..3): 3 x 4 tiles (picture, name, where / owned) of PISTOLS SMGS RIFLES, SHOTGUNS SNIPERS HEAVY, MELEE THROWABLES GADGETS; the card on the right (picture, class, where to get it, bars, numbers, a person with the spread circle at 20 m or the blast circle, the range ruler green to red, BUY / EQUIP / IN HAND, gear BUY / EQUIP). Ids `wsel_<id>`, `wpg_prev / next`, `wequip_<id>`, `wgear_<id>`, `buy_<id>` (only the selected weapon has a BUY button on screen; the tests select each tile). The loadout has an INFO button (`loadout_info`) and three mini bars.
- **Weapons.** 26 (was 18): swarm and vector (SMG), battle (rifle), sawn and auto (shotgun), bolt and dmr (sniper), knife (melee); every class has 3 to 4. Models: `convert_weapons.py` from the same CC0 Quaternius pack. Ladder routes appended after the whip, crates 3-5 are weapon crates (battle, sawn, dmr; six weapon crates), prices in `Economy.WEAPON_PRICE`. The full map labels each crate with its weapon (`MapScreen`), the ammo crates AMMO CRATE.
- **Range, target, blast in the game.** `Game.explode` now multiplies damage by `Game.blast_cover` (0.35 behind a wall, a ray from the centre to the target on `L_WORLD`). `LaunchAim` (PlayerWeapon `_launch`, only while a "throw" weapon is in hand): a ring on the ground as wide as the blast, a 14-dot arc (one MultiMesh), and for launchers (`WeaponStats.lock_on`: rocket, gravity launcher) a LOCK bracket on the enemy or car within 7.5 degrees of the crosshair (held to 12.6 degrees); `_throw` aims at the locked target. Option DAMAGE NUMBERS (Options > DATA, `UiSettings.dmg_numbers` -> `Game.damage_numbers`, default on).

## Streets, interiors and options (owner round 4; tests/test_streets_interiors_options.gd in the real scene; sheets docs/shots/slice/streets_interiors_*.jpg)
- **Furniture** (`scripts/interiors/interior_furniture.gd`, `tools/open_models/convert_furniture.py`, `assets/models/furn_*.glb`): Kenney's CC0 Furniture Kit, 38 pieces, appended to the interior's merged SURFACE builder like the Quaternius kit (no node, no draw call, the prop's DEFS box stays the collider). `RECIPES` maps a prop kind to parts [piece, fx, y, fz, fw, h, fd, yaw] in fractions of the prop's footprint (the desk is desk + monitor + keyboard + chair, the TV unit is cabinet + TV + speaker, the kitchen run is cabinet, sink, cabinet, two uppers, coffee machine and microwave); `InteriorProps.build` asks it first and falls back to the procedural boxes (`QT_FURNITURE=0` or `InteriorFurniture.enabled = false` for before / after captures). Fabric takes the room's accent and tint, wood and metal are pulled into the palette, lamps and screens are emissive. 12 new prop kinds (chair, bookcase, bookcase_wide, tv_unit, side_table, coffee_table, kitchen_run, fridge_home, coat_rack, wash_station, box_pile, tv_old, armchair) and the programs of bodega, bar, warehouse, apartment, clinic and pawn shop use them; the desk footprint grew to 1.6 x 1.2 for its chair.
- **Wall style 4** in `interior_surface.gdshader` (COLOR.a of a wall quad = 1.0): wood panelling with recessed panels to the rail and striped wallpaper above (apartment, pawn shop, clinic waiting room has tiles). Rooms people live in are lit (apartment and pawn shop ambient 0.14 / 0.15, walls 0.30).
- **Window pane** (`interior_window.gdshader`): three skylines at 22 / 46 / 95 m with their own window grids, rooftop rim light, a horizon haze and car lights sliding along the street (it was one row of big boxes).
- **Street**: `StreetDress` (one MultiMesh family, about 100 triangles per cluster: a street tree with a small crown, a slatted bench, a bin, a post box, a parking meter; its own random stream, radius 46 m, 30 % of the frontages, no collider like ShopSets). The spawn triangle budgets of test_world (MED 170k, HIGH 260k) are tight: the ShopSets radius went 80 -> 58 m to pay for it. The road lost the 6 m "second-state flicker" squares (they read as the translucent glowing rectangles of owner rounds 2 and 4) and its seam cracks are hairlines (width 0.010 + min(aa * 0.45, 0.012)).
- **Options** (`OptionsScreen`): five tabs CONTROLS, GRAPHICS, SOUND, ACCESS, GAME (the `opt_tab_N` ids and every old option id are unchanged, at most 8 rows per tab). GRAPHICS and ACCESS have a 136 px PREVIEW BAND above their rows: the graphics tab shows the tier card (`opt_tier`, a `prev` button drawn by `OptionsScreen.draw_card`: a real frame of that tier from `assets/ui/tier_<0..3>.jpg` rendered by tests/bot_slice_art.gd at LOW+ / LOW / MED / HIGH, three facts, a battery note; a tap cycles) and four chips `opt_tierset_N`; the access tab draws a subtitle plate at the chosen text and subtitle size and background plus the three marker colours as the colour-safe setting makes them. MUSIC and SFX are real sliders (`SLIDERS` field `vol:<bus>`, heard at once, saved on release; the old 100 / 75 / 50 cycle is gone), SOUND has TEST SOUND, every tab has RESET PAGE beside BACK (`reset_tab`: that tab's settings to their defaults; never progress, key binds, the language or the device's graphics tier).
- Test changes (meaning kept): test_hud (the music row is a slider: set_fraction, a tap on the track, the left end mutes), test_art_library (43 MultiMesh families: +StreetDress). New strings es / hi in strings_*_menu.gd.
- Gaps: nothing measured or looked at on a phone; the furniture is flat-colour (no textures), seat and bed meshes are the kit's stylised shapes; the civilians and the hero in the rooms are still the old blocky bodies; the street trees are small dark crowns (night, no light on them) and have no collider; hero walks through benches; the shop-window glass panes of the street facades were not touched; the tier card thumbnails are one frame (the market street) and go stale when the look changes (re-render with SLICE_SPOTS=st_w per tier, see tools in HANDOFF); the preview band is not shown for CONTROLS / SOUND / GAME.


## Interior tasks that can be finished (round 4; tests/test_interior_tasks_ui.gd, tests/test_interior_reach.gd; bots tests/interior_bot.gd, tests/room_walk.gd, tests/bot_interior_tasks.gd; sheets docs/shots/slice/interior_tasks_*.jpg)
- **Why the owner could not finish a task (found by the audit, all fixed):** (1) the quantum gate corridor (`QuantumPuzzles`) was built round the job object facing the door at any angle: in 6 of 10 office / flat seeds its walls closed the safe or the hostage in, and the verb gates carried icons only; (2) the hack, the plant and the copy had no USE prompt at all (standing in a ring did it silently); (3) a heist step in a room with no job of its own ("TAKE THE DATA") had nothing to take (it completed by a 3 s timer); (4) hidden street cars kept their collision layer: a car parked in the strip the rooms are built in was an invisible wall in the room; (5) a vendor within reach of the door hid the LEAVE prompt behind TALK; (6) nothing said what to do in a room without a job, and no exit worked from the middle of the room.
- **`InteriorTasks`** (child of `InteriorGameplay`, `ig.tasks`): one table of what a room offers. `best(pp)` = the USE action in reach (order: gate in the way, job, stealth job, heist loot, vendor / door by relative distance), `activate`, `items` (floating labels), `status_line()`, `request_leave(pp)`, `job_in_progress()`, `objective()`. Prompt texts start with the verb the touch USE button shows: OPEN, CRACK, FREE, HACK, PLANT, COPY, TAKE, TALK, LEAVE. `InteriorPrompts` (canvas layer 8, under the HUD) projects the items through the real camera: a plate over each object, the one USE would do is bright and the HUD's bottom hint stays quiet (`InteriorPrompts.active_shown`), an off-screen objective gets an edge arrow with its distance, plates never sit on the priority line.
- **Task status:** `InteriorGameplay.status_line` is the HUD plate when no job / campaign line exists (NOTHING TO DO HERE  LEAVE ANY TIME, SHOP ..., JOB DONE ..., HEIST  TAKE THE DATA, TAP LEAVE AGAIN ...). `hud_line` is unchanged (the job line only).
- **LEAVE:** touch button `leave_building` (action, key Backspace; below MORE, 92 px; hidden at the door where USE already says LEAVE and in the vendor window) and `InteriorGameplay.request_leave()`: out from anywhere; a job in progress asks to tap twice within 3 s (progress is lost on leaving, as before).
- **Jobs:** `InteriorMissions.hacking` (USE at the console, or `HACK_AUTO` 2 s in the ring with the waiting time credited, so timings are unchanged), `StealthRunner.working / prompt_for / interact / interactables` (plant, copy: same rule, `AUTO_START`), `CampaignRunner.heist_take_open() / take_data()` (heist `grace` default 3 -> 45 s: a backstop only), the stealth offer is skipped while a heist runs in that room. Objective marker: disc plus a light column (`InteriorMissions._pillar`) so the console is visible in a dark room.
- **Gates:** `QuantumPuzzles.build_fitted` / `fit_direction` / `layout` (axis directions only; the lane, the pads and the plate must stand on free floor; with the walls stamped into an `InteriorGrid` flood fill the door, the exit, the object and every pad must stay reachable, cleanest direction wins, else NO puzzle in that room: about half of the job rooms keep theirs). `assist()` / `next_gate()` / `gate_hint()`: USE near the outermost closed gate opens it (the verbs still work). The officer's room never has gates (he walks). `InteriorMissions.puzzles_on` switches them off for A/B.
- **Grid:** `InteriorGrid` (0.3 m cells from `InteriorSpace`, flood fill, gap to a point, stamp extra rects). The test-side `RoomWalk` takes the same from the real physics (hero-sized cylinder, wide planning grid, centre-line preferring search) and `InteriorBot` walks it with the touch stick (stick direction in the camera frame), touches USE / LEAVE at their drawn centre only when the touch layer shows them.
- **Not done / limits:** combat for the kill jobs is by `damage()` in the test; the safehouse is a pause-menu screen (not a room) and keeps its own test; shop UI untouched; gates are verb-explained by text now but the verbs themselves were not re-tuned; nothing measured on a phone; perf gate not re-run (no new texture, one extra draw in job rooms).


## Hero captured locomotion and weapon hold (round 4; `scripts/characters/hero_clips.gd`, `tests/test_hero_motion_hold.gd`)
- **Source:** Quaternius Universal Animation Library 1 + 2 Standard (CC0, OpenGameArt; licence and URLs in `art_sources/quaternius_universal_animation_library/`, credit in docs/THIRD_PARTY.md). The hero's skeleton and the library share the UE humanoid rest orientations, so `tools/hero/build_clips.py` retargets by the global delta of each bone from its own rest (`Q = G_anim * G_rest^-1`, half turn about Y into the model frame), writes the local rotations of 11 bones (pelvis, spine_01, spine_02, both legs with toes) plus the pelvis offset per frame at 30 fps, puts the lowest foot point 1.5 cm over the floor (the hero's sole height), re-times the loops to the left heel strike and measures the ground speed of each gait from its foot contacts. `tools/hero/clips.sh` fetches the zips, runs the converter and `tools/hero/bake_clips.gd`, which saves `assets/anim/hero_clips.res` (`HeroClipData`, 115 KB, 14 clips). Raw zips are not committed.
- **Playback (`HeroClips`, one per hero, owned by `PlayerBody.clips`):** called after `Locomotion.update` and `HeroAnimPolish.update`, before `CharacterRig.apply`. The gait phase advances by DISTANCE (`dist / stride`), so a planted foot does not slide; walk plays 0.15..2.4 m/s (clip speed 1.13 m/s), jog 4.2..6.8 (6.1 m/s), sprint 6.8+ (9.2 m/s), idle / ready (pistol-ready stance when aiming, firing or reloading) below 0.6 m/s, the jump pose in the air and a landing absorb after it. Between 2.4 and 4.2 m/s the clips do not fit (a 1.1 m/s walk and a 6.1 m/s jog), so the clip weight fades out and the procedural planted-foot IK gait (Locomotion) carries the legs; strafing and diagonals (more than ~60 degrees off the body axis) stay IK too; inside the forward and backward cones the lower body yaws toward the travel direction. The clip weight is also zero on slopes and stairs (the foot IK of the polish layer), in cover / takedown / slam / glide / wall-run / vault and in the death fall.
- **Rig contract (`CharacterRig`):** new inputs `clip_w clip_hips clip_q[11] clip_yaw clip_steady clip_counter`; with `clip_w = 0` the pose is byte-identical to before. `apply()` blends the legs with the IK legs by `clip_w` (slerp per bone), poses `spine_01` and the toes, adds the clip's `spine_02` to the Torso (held back by `clip_steady` while aiming), counters the pelvis pitch in the Torso (0.2 running, 0.75 aiming), keeps the head level against the captured spine, and takes the pelvis yaw / roll for the torso counter-rotation from the final pelvis. The arms, the weapon and the aim stay with the rig (two-handed IK on the weapon's grips).
- **Weapon hold:** `PlayerBody._solve_aim` (after pose and placement, every frame) measures the real muzzle direction against the direction from the muzzle to what the crosshair sees (the shot is the camera's ray) and feeds the residual into `rig.aim_trim` (pitch, yaw added to the rifle pose), damped, only while the weapon is raised (aiming, just fired; not while reloading or sprinting, a sprint carries it lowered). The old pitch pipeline scaled the camera pitch by 0.6, so the barrel lagged the crosshair when looking up or down; the solver removes that for every weapon and every strafe. `PlayerBody.aim_error` / `aim_solve` expose it.
- **QA:** `LocoQA` has a captured-gait mode (`clips` set, or `height_mode`): contacts from the soles' real points (heel, ball, toe tip within 3 cm of their resting height), slide = ground travel of the planted middle 60 % of each contact (heel strike and toe-off excluded) averaged over the three points, footfalls from touchdowns after a swing of 0.1 s. Measured by the test: walk 4.5, run 1.3, sprint 0.4 cm per cycle (limit 6), worst barrel error 0.9 degrees over 8 weapons x 3 pitches, worst grip error 3 cm. Look-dev: `tools/loco_view.sh OUT` (clips are on in the live Player), `tools/clip_view.sh OUT` (the hero pinned to frames of each clip), `tools/aim_view.sh OUT` (each weapon aiming / hip-ready / sprinting at several pitches with a red line from the muzzle to the crosshair target; sheet docs/shots/slice/hero_aim_sheet.jpg), `tests/bot_weapon_hold.gd` for the weapons from the real camera.
- **Gaps:** nothing between 2.4 and 4.2 m/s or in a strafe is captured (the library's Standard version has no strafes or mid-speed jog: the Pro version would fill both); death, hit reactions and crouch still use the rig's procedural poses (the crouch, death and hit clips are not wired: the death fall rotates the whole body); the jump start clip is not used (the leap is instant in the game); no clip drives the arms (the weapon is held by IK).

## Cars that are not toys, and a map that takes minutes (owner round 4; tests/test_cars_world_scale.gd in the real scene; sheets docs/shots/slice/cars_world_*.jpg)
- **Map 2.64x** (supersedes the 16 x 16 block numbers of "Big city"): `CityLayout.N` / `Game.N` 26, `HALF` 1040 (2080 m), `CORE0` 9 (the original 8 x 8 core is still in the middle, lots 0..231 unchanged), 2130 lots, 612 outer blocks with colliders streamed in chunks (about 600 blocks, never more than ~130 box shapes alive; `CityChunks`). Everything that was keyed to the old road grid adds `CityLayout.SHIFT` (= `CORE0 - 4` = 5 roads): the freedom spots / ramps (`FreedomData.junction`, clamps against `OLD_N`), weapon crates (`WeaponLadder.crate_pos`), repair bays, the forced market stalls (`StreetVendors.FORCED`) and the market avenue of the near traffic (`NearTraffic.AVENUE`); new content should use road indices relative to the core. Shader side: `W_HALF` 1040, `lot_map` 52 x 52, light map 2208 m at 3 m per texel (736 px RGBAH, 4.3 MB; was 2.5 MB), far-traffic wrap `LANE_MIN` -1060 / `LANE_LEN` 2120 (108 lanes), every fixed 1800 m AABB is now 2600 m. Map screen 52 x 52 cells of 40 m (a `map.cfg` from the 32 x 32 map migrates into the middle), minimap texture 900 px (2.4 m per pixel). Outer patrols 36 dormant groups (was 14). Build time of the city about 2x (still one frame budget at load, nothing grows at run time).
- **Speeds** (`VehicleSpecs.SPECS[kind]["max"]`, m/s): sedan 22, sport 28, compact / suv 20, taxi / wagon / luxury / ambulance 21, van 19, truck / delivery 17, bus 15, garbage 14, firetruck 19, interceptor 28, racer 29, bikes 26-28, hover 25-28. The avenue takes 72-150 s at top speed, the corner-to-corner route (2 x 2080 m) 143-300 s (a sedan 189 s); a wall at the sedan's top speed now costs about half the hull (crash curve unchanged, `test_vehicle_realism`).
- **Models** (`tools/open_models/convert_rgs_vehicles.py`, CC0 Rgsdev pack, `art_sources/rgsdev_vehicles`): `assets/models/rv_<id>.glb` for sedan, sport, compact (hatchback), suv, taxi, van, luxury (limousine), racer (roadster), interceptor (police sedan), ambulance, bus and the two new kinds `muscle` and `pickup` (also in the traffic mix). Real proportions (the Kenney set read as toys: hexagonal hubs, tall wheels, flat black windows), 0.8-1.2k triangles, flat colours, no texture memory. Contract as the other car models (Body with the surfaces Body / Details / Trim / Glass / Headlights / Taillights, WheelFL..RR at the axles, nose -Z), the pack's wheels are pushed out to the flank. `VehicleSpecs.model_names` puts `rv_` first on every tier (spec key `rv`; `QT_RV_MODELS=0` or `QT_OPEN_MODELS=0` brings the old order back for before captures and test_open_models). truck, delivery, garbage and firetruck keep their Kenney / older art (the pack's truck is a tractor unit, its firetruck has tiny wheels and 2.9k triangles: converted, tested, not shipped).
- **`CarParts`** (`scripts/vehicles/car_parts.gd`, called from `VehicleVisual._finish_car` for every four-wheeler, old art included): generated wheels (28-segment tyre with sidewall and tread, a lip, five spokes and a hub cap, a brake disc behind; shared meshes cached per radius / width, replace the model's wheel meshes in place, spin and steer as before); the windows are split off the "Details" surface by height (a `Glass` surface, per-car copy of a glossy tinted material with rim light, so the neon street and the sky reflect in it); two licence plates (8 generated 160 x 36 textures, "QT" + five digits in a 3 x 5 font, visible within 38 m); a headlight beam (two soft 10-sided cones, additive, fading at their silhouette, gated by the `night` and `wetness` globals and the car's own headlight factor through an instance uniform: one extra draw within 80 m); the cabin glow behind the glass at night (taxi 1.0, bus 1.5, ambulance 1.2, others 0.2-0.7 of a small base, none by day). Paint keeps the clear coat of `VehicleVisual` (sky radiance probes give the reflection; no ReflectionProbe, a dynamic one is too heavy for the phone budget).
- **Getting in and out**: `VehicleVisual.door_cycle(side, hold)` swings a pooled door panel (paint material + glass strip) open 66 degrees, holds and shuts (a tween on the model, played by `VehicleManager.enter` on the hero's side and by `exit_vehicle` on the exit side); `Hijack.on_exit` slides the ghost from the door to where the hero lands. A traffic car with a driver is still hijacked (driver thrown out, heat, `Hijack.hijacks`). Suspension bounce: `Vehicle._sync_body` kicks heave and pitch over road seams (`VehicleSpecs.ROAD_KICK`, spring-damper as before). Radio: `CarRadio.auto_tune()` switches the radio on at the last station when the hero gets in (toast `RADIO - NAME`), unless he cycled it to OFF himself (`muted_by_player`); the test suite (headless) skips the auto tune unless `CarRadio.auto_headless`.
- **Near traffic** (`NearTraffic.car_mesh`): the MultiMesh car is now the Rgsdev sedan decimated to 445 triangles (`tools/open_models/convert_near_car.py` -> `assets/models/rv_near.glb`, one surface, shader tags in UV.x, wheel centre in UV.y; the old 300 triangle box car is `procedural_car_mesh`, used with `QT_RV_MODELS=0` or when the file is missing). `test_slice_life` allows 520 triangles and 320 new nodes (3 plate / beam nodes per real car).
- Looking: `BOT_SET=drive BOT_OUT=/tmp/x/run BOT_RES=2400x1080 QUALITY=0 tools/vehicle_bot.sh` (the phone camera in the real city), `tools/vehicle_view.sh /tmp/x kinds=sedan,sport views=front3q,rear3q q=0`; `tools/contact_sheet.py` makes the sheets.
- Gaps: nothing measured on a real phone; no skid marks, no detaching parts; the pack's cars are flat-shaded low poly (a car is still not a photograph); the door panel is an overlay (the body mesh keeps its painted door); the hijacked driver is still a capsule (not the citizen model); the beams are a soft cone, not a light on the road (only emissive); the interior is a glow behind the glass, no seats or driver; trucks, delivery vans, garbage trucks and fire engines are still the Kenney / older art; the reflection probe of the brief was not added (cost); jobs, crates and stations were not multiplied for the new outer ring (only the 36 dormant patrols); no new sound for doors.

## Cover, side-steps, wedge and move hint (director review 4 gameplay half; tests/test_director_round4_a.gd)
- The crosshair point (`Player.get_aim().hit`) is the weapon's own ray: world and, on foot, vehicles. `AimAssist.find(origin, dir, los_from)` skips candidates `clear_line` says are hidden (at most `LOS_CHECKS` rays a frame).
- `Encounters`: no HOLD while the phase is `open` (the first wave comes at `FIRST_DELAY`); the squad ahead is scored down by `CAR_HIDDEN` when a parked car hides its spawn point (`_walled(q, ppos, mask)`).
- `Locomotion.STRAFE_CONTACT` shortens the ground contact of a sideways run; `CameraRig.INTERIOR_*` lift and lengthen the interior arm.
- `Vehicle._crash_age` + `VehicleSpecs.WEDGE_*`: a car that crawled after a crash is wedged below 1.4 m/s and backs out after 0.8 s.
- `MissionGuide._tick_move_hint`: one "how to move" line for a hero who stands still in the open with a goal on the map.
- `tests/fight_bot.gd` has `strafe_fire`; `tests/bot_slice_art.gd` has the `in_lob_a` / `in_off_*` spots.

## Rewards and progression that feed the core loop (tests/test_rewards_progression.gd, real scene; tests/bot_result_card.gd for frames; sheet docs/shots/slice/rewards_progression_cards.jpg)
- **MissionRewards** (`scripts/progress/mission_rewards.gd`, static state saved in `[rewards]`: `done`, `new_mods`; reset by `new_campaign`): `complete(kind, title)` = flat bonus (`Economy.mission_bonus`, +10 % per sector), 60 XP (`SkillTree`) and ONE unlock from a visible schedule. Missions 1..10 (`FIRST_TEN`) unlock something new EVERY time (mod, weapon, upgrade level, weapon crate = weapon + ammo + mod); after that a six-long `CYCLE` (weapon, upgrade, mod, weapon, upgrade, crate); nothing left = `CASH_FALLBACK`. The weapon is always `WeaponLadder.next_target()`, so crates and drops only bring it sooner. `missions_to_weapon()` / `next_weapon_line()` feed "NEXT WEAPON  X  IN 2 MISSIONS". `Progress.mission_result(result)` carries the dictionary (credits earned, bonus, level, XP, reward, ladder strip, upgrade hint); `Progress.earned_total` measures what the mission earned (`mark_start`).
- **MissionRewardHook** (child of Missions): listens to the completion signals of contracts, side jobs, campaign chapters (the Mission Board's missions end there), chases, stealth, heists, arena and interior jobs. Failed runs and DEMO pay nothing. A new mission system only needs to emit its existing finished signal.
- **ResultCard** (`scripts/ui/result_card.gd`, CanvasLayer 39, own hit-testing, added before the TEST menu): opens 1.4 s after a payout while Game.state is play (state paused behind it, resumed by NEXT), big credits counter, reward tile with the weapon's own picture, tiny level / XP bar, first-ten strip, next-weapon line, the guide's next objective, one contextual button (EQUIP NOW for a mod or weapon, UPGRADES when one is affordable) and a 76 px NEXT (`next_pressed`, `ResultCard.next_hook`, else `Missions.board.offer_next()`). Off under tools / tests / bots (`Game.autostart`; tests set `ResultCard.enabled`).
- **Mods as pickups**: weapon pickup ids `mod_<part>` (`Missions._collect_pickup`), ~5 % of enemy drops (`WeaponLadder.enemy_drop`, only while a mod is unowned); the LOADOUT button says NEW EQUIP until it is touched (`Progress.equip_part` clears the flag); `MissionRewards.equip_now` is the card's one tap.
- **UPGRADES**: nine ids in `Upgrades.IDS`, the flat list the player sees is `Upgrades.SIMPLE` (damage, health +15 %/level to 175, ability power +15 % damage and radius of COLLAPSE plus a teleport arrival shock, teleport range, armour); the other four (magazine, reload, regeneration, decoy time) and the SKILLS / CRAFT tabs show only with `Upgrades.show_all` (DEMO sets it; tests of those screens set it first). Health is applied by `Progress._apply_to_player` and `Player.reset`.
- **Economy**: `MISSION_BONUS`, `MISSIONS_PER_HOUR` 11, `first_ten_minutes()` (about 55), `unlock_value()` (free gear of the first ten is deducted from the full-tree time: 11.9 h), `BalanceEconomy.mission_lines()` prints the first-ten table (tools/economy_sim.sh).

## Mission Board and the easy loop (core-loop sprint; tests/test_mission_board.gd in the real scene, bot tests/bot_mission_board.gd; sheet docs/shots/slice/mission_board_before_after.jpg)
- `MissionBoard` (`scripts/missions/mission_board.gd`, `Game.missions.board`) keeps three missions on offer near the hero, drawn from sixteen types (`DEFS`: kill patrol, destroy vehicle, rescue, escort, delivery, heist, stealth, chase, convoy intercept, arena wave, boss-lite, bounty, demolition, hijack and deliver, defend, hack), plus the STORY card (the next campaign chapter, highlighted). Recipes are in `MissionBoardSteps` (2-3 campaign steps each); goals are placed by `CampaignRunner.road_point` chained from the hero. A mission is a chapter dictionary run by `CampaignRunner.start_custom` (like the heists): checkpoint at every step, `retry_pause` 1.2 s (instant retry), objective line, beacon, minimap markers, MissionGuide stuck handling all come from the runner. Pay = base + 0.55 per route metre, +10 % per tier (a tier every 4 finished missions); MissionRewards (sibling system) adds the unlock through `chapter_completed`; a finished step on EASY / NORMAL heals the hero and drops a health pack. After MAX_FAILS (5) a mission is dropped and new offers appear. `finished` / `offers_changed` signals, `offer_next()` (ResultCard NEXT calls it; without a card the board opens 2 s after the toast).
- New campaign step kinds: `smash` (break n props, nearest standing prop is the waypoint), `sneak` (reach the ring while unaware guards with a short sight keep to the other arms of the junction; heat is held at 0; any guard going aware fails the step, SPOTTED, sight shrinks after each fail). Step flags: `reach` + `car` (hijack: a car is left beside you, done only in a vehicle), `chase` + `on_foot` (parked / crawling target), any step `range` [min, max] and `quiet` (no start toast: the HUD line says it). `CampaignRunner.auto_story` (false in board mode: the story starts from the STORY card, CONTINUE or the chapter select, never by itself), `ignore_tutorial`, `retry_pause`.
- Board mode (`Missions.board_mode`, set by `board.set_take_over(true)` in the live game only, i.e. not under Game.autostart): no core hunt (no beams, no QUANTUM CORES line, no sector win by cores), random contract offers off, no chapter banner or ORACLE intro over the first job, no compass strip (line + beacon marker only), free roam shows `FREE ROAM   MAP > MISSIONS`. The first mission (`FIRST JOB`, a walk to the beacon and one lookout) starts FIRST_DELAY 3.5 s after PLAY once the prologue lines (3 s at most) are done; the first 20 s show the "how to move" line at 0.4 s, 8 s and 15 s until the hero has walked 4 m. The tutorial plate no longer holds the first step back.
- UI: `MissionBoardScreen` (`scripts/ui/mission_board_screen.gd`): `Hud._board_open`, `Hud.open_board()`, buttons `board_story`, `board_pick_0..2`, `board_diff_0..2` (EASY NORMAL HARD), `board_new`, `board_back` (FREE ROAM: always returns to play), `board_retry`, `board_abandon`; all 64 px or more. Entry points: the pause menu's MISSIONS card (`board_open`), the map's MISSIONS button (the offers are also `side` pins on the map), the result card's NEXT. `Hud.scan_hud` makes `scan_text` record the play HUD too.
- Difficulty: mode 0 is now called EASY (was STORY); `Difficulty.default_mode()` is EASY in the live game and NORMAL under tools / tests / bots, so a settings file without the key and the options RESET give EASY to players while every test keeps its numbers.
- Tests: `tests/test_mission_board.gd` (about 30 s real): pure rules, first mission within 10 s with the move line, no cores line, one objective line, the board through ESC > MISSIONS and the map, 64 px targets, the difficulty switch, the retry within 4 s, SIX DIFFERENT generated missions finished by a bot through taps on the Hud's buttons and the ResultCard's NEXT (real movement, shooting, driving: `tests/mission_audit.gd` gained smash, sneak, hijack and destroy handlers), the STORY card. Edited tests (meaning kept): test_campaign_runner (14 kinds), test_difficulty (default mode), test_hud_declutter / test_hud_gameplay (the pause menu has MISSIONS: 6 buttons).
- Gaps: the bot walks with blink hops, so the walking time of a mission on a phone (2-5 minutes) is not measured; heist and convoy are offered but the test bot prefers cheaper types; the first-launch intro cinematic (about 25 s, skippable, once) still precedes the first mission; es / hi strings for the board are missing; nothing measured on a phone; perf gate not re-run (no new draws, one UI screen).

## Menu cut, loadout, weapon wheel and map (core-loop sprint; tests/test_menu_cut.gd in the real scene; bots tests/bot_menu_shots.gd, tests/bot_wheel_shots.gd; sheets docs/shots/slice/menu_cut_*.jpg)
- **MenuFlags** (`scripts/ui/menu_flags.gd`): the cut list of docs/DESIGN.md as a flag. Default menus: title = PLAY / CONTINUE, OPTIONS, EXTRAS; pause = RESUME, MISSIONS, MAP, LOADOUT, UPGRADES (`hero_open`, the hub with UPGRADES and WEAPONS tabs), OPTIONS, EXTRAS. EXTRAS (button id `menu_gear`, page `more_back`, `Hud.more_items()`) lists SUIT, PHOTO, CHAPTERS, NEW GAME, DEMO, ABORT TO MENU and the TEST card (debug builds). The cut screens (side jobs, heists, safehouse, achievements, boss rush ARENA, and the SKILLS / CRAFT hub tabs) are offered only while `MenuFlags.show_cut()` (saved option Options > GAME > ALL EXTRAS = `UiSettings.all_extras`, or the DEMO's `Upgrades.show_all`). The code and the button ids are unchanged: `press_button(&"heist_open")` still opens the screen. Factions, collectibles and companion orders have no menu screen (HUD plate while wanted, toasts, MORE drawer).
- **LOADOUT** (`loadout_screen.gd`): three columns (weapon tiles with their own pictures, the big card, the attachment slots), squeezed horizontally by `k_of` on a 16:9 screen. The card is a 580 x 264 base px plate with the `wl_<id>.png` picture (384 x 192, `tools/icons/post.py --large`, 26 files, 108 KB, `ItemIcons.weapon_large`), name, class, numbers, four bars (damage, fire rate, range, accuracy) and `WHERE` lines (`WeaponStats.where`). One BACK; WEAPON INFO opens the catalogue on that weapon. The watchdog's global BACK still waits 30 s on screens with their own.
- **Weapon wheel** (`radial_wheel.gd`, `touch_controls.gd`): the wheel opens around the thumb (the centre of the WEAPON or ABILITY button), full circle when it fits, else a fan over the widest free arc (`open_at(..., bounds)`, `TouchControls.wheel_bounds()`), `ITEM_R` 56. While it is open the touch layer moves above the HUD layer (`layer` 11), every other button is hidden and does not answer, a 62 % scrim dims the world, release picks the dragged disc and closes it.
- **Map**: starts mostly uncharted (`START_HALF` 160 m = about 2 %, 5 % after the first open), reveals fast (`DISCOVER_R` 240 m on foot, +5 m per m/s of speed up to 420: `reveal_radius()`), draws a 96 x 96 ground texture of the district colours (`ground_texture()`, never black), a lighter veil (`FOG_ALPHA` 0.3) and a bright line around the charted region, brighter building footprints, marker badges 25 / 28 base px radius (was 17 / 19), legend chips with room for their icon.
- Looked at (render-look-fix loops, MED 1200x540 menus / LOW wheel): 1 pause UPGRADES label shrank in a 200 px card (cards 228 px); 2 loadout touched the screen edge and the picture was 220 px wide (columns recentred, plate 264 tall); 3 legend icon covered the first letter of the chip text; 4 wheel plates of the HUD layer showed through and over the discs; 5 the map's uncharted part was a black field (ground texture, lighter veil, edge line). Before / after: `docs/shots/slice/menu_cut_before_after.jpg`, `menu_cut_wheel_before_after.jpg`, `menu_cut_all_menus.jpg`.
- Tests edited (meaning kept): test_menu_declutter, test_hud_declutter, test_hud_gameplay, test_title_buttons, test_director_round2_d / 3_a / 4_b (new button lists, loadout geometry, fresh map 1-12 %), test_boss_rush / test_collectibles / test_safehouses (set `MenuFlags.all_extras` to reach the cut screens).
- Gaps: nothing measured on a phone; MED / HIGH perf runs not repeated (the map ground texture is 27 KB, the large icons load only while the loadout is open); the weapon catalogue and the GEAR / SUIT / HEIST screens keep their older layouts (single BACK, opaque plates); es / hi strings added for the new labels only; no key / pad focus order check of the new loadout; the wheel's fan has no pad path.

## A real fight (owner round 4 + director review 4 #1 / #10; tests/test_real_fight.gd in the real scene, about 25 s; bot tests/bot_real_fight.gd; sheets docs/shots/slice/real_fight_*.jpg)
- **Pacing** (`Encounters`): CONTACT IN 10 S (`FIRST_DELAY` 7.5 s + `CALLOUT_LEAD`; first aware trooper within 40 m at ~8 s), THREE waves of 3-6 troopers (`PATROL_SIZES` LOW 3/4/4, MED 4/5/6, HIGH 5/6/6 trimmed by the tier cap), then the breather (`SET_CALM`). Spawn ring 10-19 m. `Encounters.relocations`: `_free_hidden` puts a squad member who has had no line to the hero (ray from the hero's eye, world + vehicles; troopers have no collision, so one can stand inside a parked car's shape) for `HIDE_MAX` 3 s (6 s while in view) back on a road point 9-19 m away WITH a line, out of view when possible. This replaced the 20-40 s stragglers behind cars the bot found.
- **Roster look = role** (`Encounters.LOOKS`, `look_of`, `role_of`, `TacticsTable.KITS`): `EnemyManager.spawn(..., look)` / `_acquire(type, look)` reuse or build a pooled trooper of that `HumanoidVariants.VAEL_CLASSES` look (`Drone.force_look`, `look_index()`; at most six bodies per type, built under the first wave). `Operative.set_kit()` applies the kit: rifleman, heavy (gunner: 1.7 hp, slow, wide), scout (1.32 speed, flanks), grenadier and commander (throw), marksman (range 1.35, aims 1.5x longer, holds back). `TacticsTable.SQUAD_RANGE` 0.7 (they fight at 7-17 m, big enough to read) and `SQUAD_HP` 1.5 (a wave lasts 10-15 s, not 5). The director's troopers do not retreat (a wounded straggler held a wave open for 35 s); the older tactics tests are untouched (`kit_set` is false for every trooper not spawned with a look).
- **Cover that keeps him in the line of fire** (`EnemyCover.is_low_cover`, `LOW_MIN` 0.8 / `LOW_MAX` 1.3 m / radius 0.4, `find(..., low_only)`, `Operative.low_cover_only()`): the squad takes only waist to chest high cover (planter, bin, barrier, crate), never a car, van or wall; bodies thinner than 0.3 m (lamp posts, h 3-7 m) are cover for nobody in the live city. Behind it he crouches (`_crouch`: the model squashes to 70 %, head and shoulders above the cover) and stands up to fire.
- **Grenades you can see** (`EnemyGrenades`, `fx_grenade_ring.gdshader`, `EnemyManager.try_grenade / grenade_ready / grenades_thrown`): the grenadier stands 0.55 s (arm back, the muzzle flare swells), then a glowing ball flies in an arc (0.7-1.35 s) to where the hero is walking (`lead_point`), a red ring on the street as wide as the blast (3.6 m) burns down over the flight and the 0.85 s fuse, then `Game.explode`. At most 1 (LOW / MED) or 2 (HIGH) live, 2 pooled slots, the physics process is off while none is live. First throw 1-2.2 s after contact, then every 6-9 s, squad gap 4 s.
- **Readability at 20-40 m**: `Drone.update_lod` sets the instance shader parameter `far_k` (hero_body.gdshader: a body-wide glow in the trim colour, strongest at grazing angles, 9 -> 30 m, aware troopers only, written only when it changes by 0.02) and `_dist_scale` (1.0 up to 12 m, `DIST_SCALE_MAX` 1.35 at 38 m; the hit shape `hit_ray` / `body_box` grows with it, so a shot at the bigger figure lands); `HumanoidVariants.VAEL_BIG` 1.12; `EnemyMarks.RING_SQUAD` 14 -> 34 m (the ring covered the figure). Hit flash: `hit_k` (white-hot body for ~0.12 s on every hit). No new draw, mesh or texture.
- **The hero** (`tests/real_fight_bot.gd`, shared by the test and the frame bot): strafes while he fires (direction every 0.45-0.9 s), backs off inside 6.5 m, drops a target that ducks behind a car (the old bot kept shooting a parked van), turns to the nearest threat and walks in only when it is farther than 22 m.
- **Frames**: `FIGHT_OUT=/tmp/f FIGHT_SECONDS=75 BOT_FASTEXIT=1 BOT_USER_ARGS="--touch" BOT_RES=1200x540 QUALITY=0 ENGINE_ARGS="--fixed-fps 12" BOT_TIMEOUT=590 tools/bot.sh res://tests/bot_real_fight.gd /tmp/x 100000 1` (about 3.5 min LOW; `FIGHT_LINE=aware` roster line-up 7-33 m; `FIGHT_CLOSE=1` three troopers at 11-18 m, a frame every 0.4 s; `k_<t>.png` = 0.5 s after each kill; `FIGHT_KILLS=0` turns those off).
- Looked at (render-look-fix loops, LOW 1200x540 / 1600x720 / 2400x1080 and MED 1200x540, every frame opened): 1 baseline: 20-60 px dark figures with pink rings, hero planted, wave at 18 s; 2 role-coloured glow + rings from 34 m (figures finally read, but the rings still covered them); 3 grenade ring and ball (ring is a red swoosh round the hero, readable); 4 hero bot walked into stalls and cars (bot movement tuned, camera clipping into a car window is the camera's, not this wave's); 5 range 0.7, hp 1.5, hidden-trooper fix, bigger Vael. Before / after: `docs/shots/slice/real_fight_before_after.jpg`, `real_fight_roster_med.jpg`.
- Tests edited (meaning kept, new numbers): test_slice_fight (3-6 troopers, contact about 10 s), test_combat_feel (3-6; engaged share of the 60 s window 0.6 -> 0.35 because three waves now end in about 45 s), test_director_round3_a (first squad at 6-20 s).
- Perf (PERF_CHECK=1 tools/perf_report.sh 0 1 2): MED 96 visible / 128 total draws, 166 MB, death 143; HIGH 114 / 149, 221 MB, death 165: OK. Grenade slots add two draws only while a grenade is live.
- Gaps: nothing measured on a phone; the figures at 25-40 m are still small (camera distance and FOV are the camera's); the hero strafe pose is a wide lunge and the camera clips into cars when the bot walks into them (not this wave's files); the grenade has no sound of its own (reuses enemy_shot / impact_metal); enemy cry / bark lines are not added; the crouch is a model squash, not an animation; trooper look builds happen on the first wave (not pre-warmed in `warm_begin`); no es / hi strings (no new UI text); MED / HIGH 90 s fight runs were not repeated (MED 34 s only).


## Weapon roster (owner round 5: COD + GTA weapon types, generic names, six quantum weapons; tests/test_weapon_roster.gd in the real scene; sheets docs/shots/slice/weapon_roster_*.jpg)
- **One table** (`WeaponRoster`, scripts/player/weapon_roster.gd): 35 slots (pistol, heavy pistol, revolver, machine pistol, 2 SMG, 3 assault rifles, battle rifle, LMG, minigun, pump and auto shotgun, sniper, anti-material rifle, crossbow, RPG, grenade launcher, frag / smoke / flash grenades, C4 with detonator, proximity mine, flamethrower, stun gun, katana, knife, bat, 6 quantum) mapped to the real objects: `WeaponDefs` (guns and melee, now 43), `Gear.THROWABLES` (6, new FLASH GRENADE) and `Gear.CHARGES` (C4, numbers mirror `Charges`). `model / icon / sound / stat_lines / unlock / trait_text / tabs` answer the five questions the test asks of every entry (model, picture, stats, sound, way to get it).
- **17 new weapons** (`WeaponDefs`): magnum (HAMMER HEAVY PISTOL), mpistol, stun, ranger, bullpup (3-round burst), lmg, amr, xbow, flame, katana, bat and QUANTUM class: qsuper (SUPERPOSITION GUN: a shot and its echo), qlink (ENTANGLE LINKER: the same damage to a second enemy up to 40 m away), qcollapse (COLLAPSE CANNON: 10.5 m violet blast), qblink (BLINK PISTOL, alt-fire = teleport to the crosshair), qphase (PHASE RIFLE: through 3 walls), qstasis (STASIS RAY: freezes). New def keys read by `PlayerWeapon`: `silent` (no noise for the AI), `wall` + `wall_k` (rounds pass that many walls, never the ground, each costs wall_k of the damage), `stun` (seconds frozen through `Drone.disable`, 30 % on bosses), `burn` + `burn_time` (damage over time, `_tick_burn`), `flame` (fire puffs instead of a tracer); alt kind `blink`. Counters for tests: `wall_hits stuns burn_ticks blinks`.
- **Classes and tabs**: `WeaponDefs.CLASS_ORDER` gained QUANTUM (8 wedges on the radial wheel, an empty class has no wedge, there is no quantum starter). The LOADOUT is one tab per owned class (`LoadoutScreen.owned_classes / page_count`, the arrows flip the class, the caption says "RIFLE 2 / 6"); the WEAPONS catalogue has five pages (PISTOLS SMGS / RIFLES SHOTGUNS / SNIPERS HEAVY / MELEE QUANTUM / EXPLOSIVES GADGETS) and shows the trait line (SILENT, SHOOTS THROUGH 3 WALLS, FREEZES 2.5 S, SETS ON FIRE, ECHO SHOT, ALT = TELEPORT).
- **Getting them** (`WeaponLadder`, 36 entries, one free route each): 5 new crates (RIVER, CORNER, ROOFTOP, RIFT, ECHO), campaign chapters 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12 (one weapon per chapter), enemy drops (flanker, shield, brute, striker, sniper), the old boss drops, shop. New prices are low (Economy.WEAPON_PRICE) so the full gear tree stays at 12.7 h (test_balance2 band 8-13).
- **Models and pictures**: six open models (`convert_weapons.py`, CC0 Quaternius, credits in docs/THIRD_PARTY.md), the rest procedural in `WeaponModels` (new `_Builder.tube` / `quad` make hollow emitter rings; solid discs read as white blobs). Icons `w_ / wl_ / g_` for all new items (tools/icons/build_icons.sh only=..., then post.py --large; `g_c4`, `g_flash`).
- **Sound**: 39 new OGG takes (`make_real.py build_roster`, from clips already in the repo plus a synthesised crossbow twang, stun crackle and flame whoosh): every new weapon has its own sound name (`shot_magnum`, `shot_mp`, `shot_ar2`, `shot_bpup`, `shot_lmg`, `shot_amr`, `shot_xbow`, `shot_flame`, `shot_stun`, `melee_katana`, `melee_bat`, `shot_qsuper|qlink|qcollapse|qblink|qphase|qstasis`), throwables and C4 carry a `snd` key. OGG keeps the audio folder inside its 24 MB test budget.
- **HUD**: the weapon pop card puts long names on two lines, the ammo box shrinks "RELOADING" to fit, the loadout tile caption uses the first word when the last is ambiguous (three "PISTOL" tiles).
- Gaps: the roster is not balanced against real play (only the DPS band of BalanceModel); the quantum weapons share the sparse existing blast elements; the flamethrower has no ground fire; PHASE and AMR rounds also pass vehicles; no weapon-specific reload animation or inspect pose for the new models; melee weapons reuse the blade/knife swing; nothing measured on a phone (perf gate not run: the new meshes are one draw each, the same as before).


## Vehicle roster: any vehicle, GTA types (owner round 5, docs/DESIGN.md; tests/test_vehicle_roster.gd in the real scene, about 45 s; bot tests/bot_craft.gd; sheets docs/shots/slice/vehicle_roster_*.jpg)
- **The roster** (`VehicleSpecs.ROSTER`, `NAMES`, `name_of`): compact, sedan, sports, muscle, SUV, van, pickup, bus, taxi, police car, ambulance, fire truck, ARMOURED TRUCK, motorbike, QUAD, SCOOTER, hover bike, HELICOPTER, SMALL PLANE, SPEEDBOAT, TANK, plus the older limo, racer, wagon, truck, delivery, garbage truck, hover car, quantum scooter, skiff and rover. Every kind has a picture (`assets/icons/v_<kind>.png`, `tools/icons/build_icons.sh`, the icon maker now walks `ROSTER`), a plain name, an engine family (`AudioDirector.ENGINES`) and a model: Rgsdev / Kenney art for the cars, open CC0 models for the craft (`assets/models/xv_heli|plane|boat|tank.glb`, `tools/open_models/convert_craft.py`, credits in docs/THIRD_PARTY.md, licences in art_sources/open_craft), procedural bodies for the quad and the scooter and the armour plates of the armoured truck (`VehicleCraftModels`, one `Body` mesh with Body / Glass / Trim / Chrome surfaces and wheel nodes, no texture).
- **Handling classes** (`spec["cls"]`): `car`, `bike`, `hover` as before, plus `quad` (the arcade model, four wheels), and the craft classes with their own physics in `VehicleCraft` (`craft` spec key, `Vehicle.craft: VehicleCraft.State`, null for everything else): `heli`, `plane`, `boat`, `tank`. `Vehicle.step_drive` hands a craft to `VehicleCraft.step`, an unmanned one to `VehicleCraft.idle` (a helicopter sinks, a plane glides down, a boat drifts), `_sync` to `VehicleCraft.sync` (blades, attitude, bob, wake, turret). `Vehicle.base_y` is the rest height (0, a boat floats at `FloodZone.LEVEL - draft`).
- **Controls** (touch and keys share the actions): stick forward / back = throttle or forward speed, left / right = yaw, UP (`jump`, the JUMP button reads UP) climbs or pulls the nose up, DOWN (`crouch`, a second button shown only for aircraft) descends, `fire` shoots the mounted weapon, VIEW and BACK as in a car. Cars keep DRIFT; a boat and a tank read BRAKE.
- **Helicopter**: rotor spools up in 1.6 s (`SPOOL_READY` 0.85 lets it lift), climbs 11 m/s, descends with a soft flare (`_flare`: the last metres follow sqrt(2 a h) so a landing never hurts), hovers when the stick is left alone, yaws 1.7 rad/s, 46 m/s top speed, skids at 5 m/s on the ground. **Plane**: the stick forward / back moves a throttle LEVER that stays where it is left; it must reach `stall` (24 m/s) x 0.9 before UP lifts the nose off; below the stall speed it sinks; a climb costs speed, a dive gains it; landing is the same flare, then stick back brakes on the wheels. Both are moved by `move_and_collide` in 3D against buildings and roofs (a roof is a landing field; `State.agl` is the ray-measured height above whatever is below, used for the flare and the HUD), a hard landing (> `LAND_SAFE` 6.5 m/s) and a wall hit (> 5 m/s) hurt. `can_exit`: an aircraft can be left only when down and slow (`LAND TO GET OUT`). Aloft they ram nothing and the car-to-car bump ignores them (`Vehicle.is_aloft`).
- **Boat**: floats on the FLOODED QUARTER (`FloodZone`: the 4 x 3 blocks of streets south of the core, x -170..170, z 390..650, water 0.5 m above the street, one translucent plane with `flood_water.gdshader`, no collision changes), arcade drift model with a bow-up pitch, bob and roll, wake bursts, kept inside the rect. **Tank**: tracks turn it on the spot, 12.5 m/s, hp 2000, 42 t (it pushes cars away), the turret slews at 1.7 rad/s to the crosshair (`Player.get_aim()`, or the `aim_at` meta in tests) and the TANK CANNON (blast 7 m) fires along the turret.
- **Weapons** (`VehicleSpecs.WEAPONS`): MINIGUN (heli), WING GUNS (plane), DECK GUN (boat), TANK CANNON; `VehicleWeapons` fires along `Vehicle.aim_dir()` (the nose, or the line to the crosshair kept inside `aim_cone`) from `Vehicle.muzzle_pos()`. HUD: `Hud.vehicle_rows` / `_draw_vehicle_readout` is a small plate over the speedometer (ALT, THROTTLE, the next thing to do: PULL UP TO TAKE OFF / STALLING / HOLD UP TO LIFT OFF, and the gun with its rounds); the chase camera reads `cam_d` / `cam_h` of the spec.
- **Spawn rules**: `VehicleSpawns.POINTS` (helipad on a core plaza, airstrip on the east avenue, a tank on the west ring road, three boat slips in the flooded streets, map markers with the vehicle name): a point with nobody on it and the player within 100 m gets a fresh vehicle (`VehicleManager._tick_spawn_points`, once a second, uses a free slot or the farthest car's), a destroyed one returns after 40 s, one the player drove away is replaced when he comes back. The armoured truck (1.2 %), quad (2.5 %) and scooter (3 %) are rare street traffic (`STREAM_KINDS`). Random cars never spawn in the flooded quarter.
- Tests edited (meaning kept): test_cars_world_scale (the one-minute-per-avenue rule is for ground vehicles, aircraft excluded).
- Gaps: nothing measured on a phone; the aircraft have no cockpit view or landing gear animation; the boat has no deck hero and no splash on the hero wading; the hero cannot yet bail out of a flying craft (no parachute); only one boat model; engines reuse the existing loops (no rotor or propeller sound); traffic cars still drive through the flooded streets; helicopter and plane paints are random from a small palette; es / hi strings for the new names.


## Gameplay fixes after director review 5 (tests/test_director_round5_a.gd in the real scene; docs/SLICE_REVIEW.md "Fix pass, review 5, gameplay half")
- **Training vs the board**: `Tutorial.board_owns_goals()` (Missions.board_mode, the live game): `maybe_start` returns false, a pill already up ends itself when a board job runs. The first job is the training.
- **Arena**: board `survive` steps carry `near` / `far` (spawn ring, default 36-58) and `clear` (the clock alone does not end the step: `CampaignRunner._tick_survive` waits for the foes, `SURVIVE_CLEAR_GRACE` 40 s). `MissionBoardSteps` arena: 3 + tier foes every 9 s from 18-34 m, hold 44 s. Offers carry `gift` (`MissionRewards.gift_text()`), appended to the brief.
- **Vendors**: `AmbientCity.civilian_ray` finds a pinned stall vendor up to `VENDOR_SLACK` 2.2 m past the first obstacle (his counter); `AmbientCrowd.is_vendor(i)`.
- **Job room**: `InteriorPlan` lamp pool of every room behind the street room hangs at `JOB_LAMP_Y` 2.3 m with `JOB_LAMP_REACH` 2.2 m more radius; `interior_surface.gdshader` dims ceiling strips near the lens and takes 40 % of the pool light on the ceiling; `QuantumPuzzles` corridor walls use `quantum_wall.gdshader` (panels, seams, hazard stripe, cyan trim); `InteriorMissions._make_safe` builds a vault safe.
- **Render glitches**: `Missions.PICKUP_MODEL_SCALE` (health / energy pickups), `MissionVisuals.BEAM_SHADER_CODE` silhouette falloff and near fade, `LightingPolish.CONE_SHADER` falloff.
- **Feel**: `CombatStep.EASE` (the side-step speed eases through a reversal), `PlayerBody.HEAVY_LOOK_SCALE` / `HEAVY_IDS` (heavy guns drawn bigger).

## Review 5 presentation fixes (builder note; tests/test_director_round5_b.gd)
- Camera: `CameraRig._vehicle_avoid` (second ray head to lens on `L_VEHICLE`, `VEHICLE_CLEAR` / `VEHICLE_LIFT`); the spring arm itself still ignores cars, so a hero walking behind a car keeps his view.
- Menus: `MissionBoardScreen.card_w` (cards up to 1120 base px) and the pay chip; `Hud.MORE_W` 420; `TestMode.entry_shown()` decides whether TEST is visible (the hooks stay on `available()`).
- Weapon wheel: wedge items carry `cname` / `wname`; `TouchControls._draw_wheel` draws the picture large, the class in words, a count badge and a name plate.
- Map: `MapScreen` merges overlapping badges after the per-kind clusters (always-kinds never merged); `drawn["badges"]` lists what was drawn; caps 14 / 20 / 24.
- Lamp cones: ten faces with normals (`LightingPolish.build_cones`), silhouette fade in `CONE_SHADER`.

## Water world (wave 104; scripts/world/terrain_height.gd, water_layout.gd, water_world.gd, scripts/player/player_swim.gd; tests/test_water_world.gd, look-dev tests/water_view.tscn and tests/bot_water.gd)
- **TerrainHeight is the source of truth for the ground** (the terrain builder's `TerrainBase.height` is its `base_height`): `height_at(x, z)` = land with rivers and lakes carved in (`WaterLayout.carve`), `water_level_at` / `water_level_for` / `depth_at` / `is_water` / `flow_at` / `slope_at`, `SEA_LEVEL` -2 (the city plateau is y 0), `NO_WATER` sentinel. `TerrainHeight.warm()` once on the main thread before any threaded sampling. A height sample is about 10 us (rivers cost a dictionary lookup in a 64 m grid), so never sample per pixel.
- **WaterLayout**: 3 rivers (HIGHLINE RIVER into the west sea, EASTWATER into the south sea, NORTH CREEK, a tributary of the first) and 3 lakes (MIRROR LAKE, QUARRY LAKE, HIGH TARN) as data in `RIVERS` / `LAKES`. A river is a Catmull-Rom spline sampled every 12 m, nudged into the nearest valley, then given a surface that never rises downstream (running minimum of the land under the centre line, smoothed, landing on the sea level or on the river it joins over the last 300 m), widths and depths growing to the mouth, a current speed from the fall (0.8-4.5 m/s). Bed = parabola, banks rise 0.22 and blend into the land over `bank` metres (wider where the river cuts through high ground, so gorges get walls). A river that does not reach the sea after the land was reshaped is carried on along its last heading (<= 900 m) until it does; a tributary never runs uphill to its river. Lakes: level = lowest rim land - 0.25, parabola bowl. Changing `base_height` re-cuts everything at the next start, no hand work.
- **WaterWorld** (Node3D under the city: `City._ready` calls `WaterWorld.attach(self)`; `WaterWorld.instance`): TWO draw calls, no textures but a 512 x 512 R8 shore depth map (about 260 KB, `shore_map()`: 128 x 128 coarse pass over the 6.4 km world, refined x4 only where the ground is within -4.5..+7 m of the sea; built once, about 0.5 s on a desktop). Ocean = one radial mesh (centre fan + geometric rings, 3 m .. 3.4 km) re-centred on the camera every frame; `water_ocean.gdshader`: four sine waves (vertex height AND analytic normal, amplitude 0.32 m, fading with distance and dying over the last 5 m of depth), two drifting noise layers for ripples (HIGH), depth colour from the shore map (turquoise to deep blue), see-through alpha at the waterline, breaking foam lines and a thin foam edge, whitecaps (HIGH), slow patches of tone, fresnel towards a sky colour, sun / moon glint from the engine's own specular and sky radiance, a dark glassy ceiling seen from below. Rivers and lakes = one mesh (`build_inland_mesh`): river-space UVs (metres along, metres across), UV2 = (speed / 5, bank), TANGENT = flow, so ripples are noise in river space advected downstream (`water_inland.gdshader`): stretched along the flow, fast water gets white streaks, banks get a foam edge, shallows turn turquoise. Tiers (`TIERS`): ocean segments x rings 28 x 20 / 48 x 26 / 72 x 32, river sample skip 4 / 2 / 1, cross vertices 3 / 3 / 5, shader `detail` 0 / 1 / 2; water triangles about 1.8k / 4k / 10k (`triangle_count()`); `QT_NO_WATER=1` skips the water for perf A/B runs; a tier change rebuilds the two meshes. No allocation per frame: two uniform writes (`wtime`) and a position.
- **CPU side of the same waves** (boats, swimmers, effects): `WaterWorld.wave_height(x, z, t)` is the shader's four-wave sum (keep `WAVE_*` and `waves()` identical), `surface_y(x, z)` (sea waves, flat rivers and lakes, `NO_WATER` on land), `buoyancy(pos, yaw, half_len, half_wid)` returns {water, y (heave), pitch, roll, flow, depth} for a hull (wave 105 plugs boats in here instead of FloodZone.LEVEL), `splash(pos, strength)` (droplets via FX.burst, a ripple ring via FX.marks.ripple, a sound: wade < 0.55 <= splash < 1.6 <= big).
- **Underwater**: when the viewport camera is below `surface_y` the `UnderwaterTint` CanvasLayer (layer -1: over the 3D view, under every HUD layer) shows a blue tint with vignette and a low-pass is enabled on the Master bus (`_set_lowpass`); `WaterWorld.underwater` is the flag. The hero's own camera stays above the water while he floats.
- **Sounds** (CC0, OpenGameArt, `art_sources/audio_water`, built by `tools/audio/make_water.py` into `assets/audio/water_*.ogg`): `water_splash` (4), `water_splash_big` (2), `water_wade` (3), `water_stroke` (3), `water_bubble`, and two loops that `WaterWorld` plays itself with a volume from the distance to the sea (8 probes at 4 radii, 4 times a second) / to rivers and lakes (`WaterLayout.inland_distance`): `water_surf`, `water_river`.
- **PlayerSwim** (`player.swim`, called from `Player._foot_tick` before the ground movement): water over the feet > 0.3 m = wading (speed x0.62, a splash and a rustle per step, `wade_mult` is read by the ground speed), > 1.25 m = swimming (leaves again below 0.95): he floats with the water at his chest (`FLOAT_DEPTH` 1.1, a spring on the surface including waves), swims at 3.3 m/s (sprint = crawl 5.6) along the stick, is carried by the river's current, JUMP hops (0.4 s of free flight, clears a ledge), landing from a height plunges him in (the fall is eaten by the water, a big splash), strokes play a sound and a ripple ring. Oxygen-free: nobody drowns. Weapons work while swimming. `PlayerBody` pitches the body forward with the crawl (`swim.lean`), rolls it with the strokes and bobs it with the waves; the legs keep their gait as a kick.
- Gaps: no dive (the camera never goes under except by teleport); no underwater fog change; boats still use FloodZone (hook ready: `buoyancy`); no wake or bow wave; rain rings on the sea are not drawn; vehicles driving into water just drive through it (no sinking); the ocean does not reflect terrain (sky radiance only); the shore map needs a rebuild when `base_height` changes at run time (it is baked once per session); NPCs and enemies ignore water.


## Terrain world (round 5 terrain foundation; scripts/terrain/*, assets/shaders/terrain*.gdshader; tests/test_terrain_world.gd, real scene; look-dev bot tests/bot_terrain.gd)
Owner round 5: "mountains outside the city, ocean, rivers, as big as possible". The world is a 6 km square (`TerrainHeight.WORLD_HALF` 3000); the 2.2 km city sits on a flat plateau (`CITY_HALF` 1100, y = 0) in the middle of an island: the highways leave through the four gates into forest hills (north-west and west), dry plains and desert (east and south) and the mountain range (north and north-east, peaks about 400 m, snow above `TerrainBase.SNOW_LINE` 250 m); the coast and the open sea are on the south-west and beyond every shore (`SEA_LEVEL` -2). Rivers, lakes and the water meshes belong to the water layer (scripts/world/water_layout.gd, water_world.gd, another builder); the contract between the two is `scripts/world/terrain_height.gd` (`height_at`, `base_height`, `water_level_at`, `depth_at`, `slope_at`, `warm`).
- **One height function, three layers** (`TerrainHeight.height_at` = `TerrainRoads.carve(WaterLayout.carve(TerrainBase.height(x, z)))`): the LAND (`TerrainBase`: fixed-seed FastNoiseLite, forest / desert weights by compass sector, a domain-warped ridged range with crags, the beach and sea-floor profile from `TerrainHeight.coast_distance`), then the rivers and lakes, then the ROAD BENCHES. Pure functions of (x, z): meshes, colliders, vehicles, the hero's feet, the map picture and the wildlife to come all ask it, nobody keeps a copy. `TerrainHeight.warm()` (main thread, once: A* routes about 0.4 s on a desktop) before worker threads sample. Cost about 10 us per sample.
- **Roads** (`TerrainRoads`): four highways out of the city gates (PASS ROAD north, DESERT ROAD east, COAST ROAD south, FOREST ROAD west) and two dirt tracks that branch off them (MOUNTAIN TRACK, QUARRY TRACK). Each is routed by A* on a 40 m lattice in a corridor around its via points (grade limit 7 % highway / 14 % track, steep and wet cells cost extra, a road that cannot reach its goal ends where it gets closest: the dead end at the foot of the range), relaxed, spline-resampled every 10 m, given a vertical PROFILE (smoothed land, cut and fill under the grade limit per metre, start height fixed at the gate or on the parent road, a causeway 1.3 m over any river it crosses) and a flat BENCH 15 m (highway) / 8 m (track) either side of the centre line blending over 30 / 16 m (`carve`). `nearest()` uses a 64 m cell grid (one dictionary lookup outside a road corridor). `build_ribbon(i)` makes the visible ribbon (one mesh per road, y = terrain + 0.07, flared to the 20 m city road at a gate); shader `terrain_road.gdshader`: asphalt, double yellow centre, white edge lines, dashed lane marks, wear; dirt tracks use the gravel layer with wheel ruts and soft edges. `name_at`, `on_road`, `shoulder_weight` for the map, grip and the shader.
- **Streaming** (`TerrainWorld`, child `terrain` of the City): chunks of 250 m. LOD0 = 32 cells (7.8 m), the 3 x 3 chunks around the hero, with colliders (ONE `ConcavePolygonShape3D` per chunk made from the very same triangles as the mesh, layer world); LOD1 = 16 cells (15.6 m) out to R1 chunks (LOW 2, MED 3, HIGH 4); LOD2 = 32 far tiles of 1000 m (31 m cells) built once for the whole world (about 82 k triangles, a few draws visible at a time) that cut the square the complete near chunks cover out with a shader rectangle (`hole`) and sit 2.5 m low, so the near chunks always win at the seam. Every chunk has a skirt (6 / 14 / 70 m) against cracks between levels; normals come from central differences one sample beyond the square, so lighting is continuous across chunks and levels. Heights are sampled on WorkerThreadPool tasks (LOD0 about 23 ms, LOD1 6 ms, far tile 14 ms each on a desktop), meshes are uploaded on the main thread with a per-tier budget (uploads per frame LOW 1 / MED 2 / HIGH 3, jobs in flight 2 / 3 / 4). Nothing is built inside the plateau (chunks and tiles that lie wholly inside it are skipped; the shader discards the overlap) so the city centre costs nothing. Focus = the hero (the camera without one). Shutdown rule: a worker task that runs script code while the engine quits crashes it (signal 11 after RESULT: PASS), so `TerrainWorld._exit_tree` waits for every job and `TerrainMap.shutdown()` cancels and waits for the map picture; any new worker-thread feature must do the same. `settle(pos)` builds everything around a point at once (tests), `ensure_ground(pos)` the LOD0 ring only (the City calls it from `update_colliders`, which every teleport already calls); `_rescue_player` puts a hero that ended up under the terrain back on it.
- **Look** (`terrain.gdshader`): ground by the vertex colour (r forest, g desert, b shoreline, a road verge; `TerrainBase.shade_weights`), rock by slope and altitude (tri-planar on the steep parts), snow above the snow line with a ragged noise edge that stays off steep faces, gravel verge, a second tap at an unrelated scale per ground layer against tile repeat (MED and HIGH), night lift from the global `night`, wetness from `wetness`. The seven Poly Haven CC0 layers (grass, forest, desert, beach, rock, snow, dirt) are ONE Texture2DArray (`TerrainTextures`): HIGH 1024 px (9.3 MB), MED and LOW the 512 px level (2.3 MB, the top mip is cut off the compressed data); without the array (headless) flat biome colours are used. Fog: `TerrainWorld.fog_zone` (0 in the city, 1 more than 500 m outside the plateau) scales the Environment's density to 30 % (`Weather._apply_env`), so the mountains read; the sky shader's painted skyline is off (`skyline_amount` 0, the terrain is the horizon). `TerrainRidges`: one ring mesh of far ranges at 8.2 km around the camera (unshaded, haze colour of the fog, one draw call) for the horizon over the sea; the gameplay camera's far plane is 12 km.
- **The city and the world** (shared edits): the city's invisible border walls are gone; walls stand at `CityLayout.PLAY_HALF` (2960). `CityLayout.WALK` is now only the CITY square (patrols, mission tables, interiors, `Drone.WORLD_LIMIT`: enemies stay in the city). The city ground plane is 2.2 km (the plateau), the ground collision box ends 6 m past it, the far skyline is two blocks beyond the border (624 towers), `City.ground_height` returns the terrain outside the plateau, `PlayerUtil.safe_spot` / `outside_city` use the whole world (floor from the terrain outside the city, rescue only beyond the world walls or far under the terrain), `VehicleManager._sanity` and the exit-spot picker too.
- **Vehicles**: outside the plateau a car follows the terrain (four corner samples, eased), its model pitches and rolls with the slope (`ground_pitch` / `ground_roll`), the hit box is lifted 0.45 m so the terrain colliders (world layer) do not hold it like a wall (a slope steeper than about 25 % does), it never drives into deep water (`depth_at` > 1.2 m: it stops at the shore). `surface` (asphalt, dirt, grass, sand, rock, snow, water; `TerrainWorld.surface_at`, refreshed 4 times a second for the driven car) feeds `VehicleSpecs.surface_grip` (tyre mu) and `surface_speed` (top speed): an off-road build (`VehicleSpecs.OFFROAD`: rover 1.0, quad 1.0, tank 1.0, SUV 0.88, truck 0.85) keeps 74 to 90 % of its grip on dirt, sand and rock, a street car 42 to 60 %. Parked 4x4s wait at the gates (`VehicleSpawns` outpost_rover, outpost_suv, outpost_quad). Traffic stays on the city grid: nothing streams a car outside the plateau (`road_point_near` finds no road there); only the hero's car and the parked outposts are out there.
- **Maps**: `TerrainMap` is ONE 512 px picture of the whole world (hill-shaded biome colours, snow, sea, roads), built once on a worker thread (a couple of seconds on a phone) and installed by `poll()`; the minimap draws it under the city layer (the city layer is clipped to its own square, `Minimap._clip_unit`), the map screen draws it under the city and replaces the old faint outskirts grid, and the zoom button gets a fourth level WORLD (`MapScreen.set_world_mode`, zoom 0.36: the whole 6 km in the screen height, region names from `TerrainMap.LABELS`, panning over the whole world, `map_to_world` clamps to the world); `cycle_zoom` keeps its three levels, pinching out stops at the city overview.
- **Budgets**: terrain texture memory +2.3 MB on LOW and MED, +9.3 MB on HIGH (the limits LOW 200 / MED 260 / HIGH 360 MB are not raised); draws: chunks and tiles are separate MeshInstance3Ds with ONE shared material per kind (2 terrain materials, 2 road materials, 1 ridge ring), only those in the frustum are drawn; triangles at the city edge LOD0 9 x 4.6 k + LOD1 up to 40 x 1.2 k + far tiles 32 x 2.6 k worst case with everything in view (about 160 k; looking along a highway about 60 k). `TerrainWorld.stats()` reports chunks, bodies, tris, nodes and the main-thread upload time.
- **Gaps**: no vegetation, rocks or animals yet (the next waves: scatter by biome from `TerrainBase.shade_weights` / `biome_at`); bridges over the rivers (the roads cross them on causeways, a ford at most); the world map shows no fog of war outside the city; enemies and civilians do not leave the city; the sea edge has the wall at 2960 m but no shore line mesh of its own (the water layer draws the sea); LOW has no per-pixel second tap; the far tiles are 2.5 m low so a river on a far tile can float above its banks (hidden by the fog); no test of the Compatibility renderer path of the terrain shader (flat colours are used without the array).


## Bridges, harbour and boats (wave 105; scripts/world/bridges.gd, bridge_kit.gd, bridge_world.gd, bridge_traffic.gd, harbour.gd, harbour_kit.gd, harbour_world.gd, boat_run.gd, scripts/vehicles/vehicle_craft.gd; shaders harbour_kit / harbour_ground; test tests/test_bridges_harbour_boats.gd in the real scene; look-dev `tools/bot.sh res://tests/bot_terrain.gd` with `TERR_VIEWS`)
- **Bridges are placed by the road network.** `TerrainRoads._build_road` marks every run of road samples that has open river or lake water within the carriageway (plus 2 samples each side) as a SPAN (`road["spans"]`, `_sb`): the bench is NOT cut there (`carve` returns the river land, `nearest()` has a 4th element = span flag, `TerrainRoads.in_span(x, z)`), the ribbon skips the span, and the profile clears the water by `BRIDGE_CLEAR` 3.6 m (was a 1.3 m causeway that dammed the river). `Bridges.ensure()` (data only) turns the spans into bridges (`Bridges.list`: id, name from the river or lake, kind road / track, pts = the road samples with the profile as y, w, road_w, len, piers) and adds the HARBOUR GATE suspension bridge (z 1604, x -250..380, deck 9 m over the span, towers on the quay edges), two footbridges (EASTWATER, NORTH CREEK, found from the river paths) and the HIGHLINE rail trestle (two tracks, 12 m over the gorge; the railway itself is a later wave). Six road/track bridges at the moment (PASS, DESERT and FOREST roads, three on the MOUNTAIN TRACK including a 158 m one over QUARRY LAKE).
- **Queries**: `Bridges.deck_y(x, z, y_hint)` (deck height for a thing at y_hint, NONE when not over a deck or more than 3 m under it), `on_deck`, `nearest(p)`, `y_at(b, u)`, `point_at(b, u)`, `TerrainHeight.drive_height_at(x, z, y_hint)` = the deck or the ground. Vehicles use it: `Vehicle._terrain_step` samples its four corners with it and skips the deep-water rule over a deck, `VehicleManager._sanity` and `_exit_point` too (a car on a deck 5 m over the river bed was being "recovered" to the bed). Cars follow the deck height; the hero walks on the deck colliders.
